L\inf_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle W^{1}\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)}}{{\psi_{1}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W^{1})}}}\frac{\int_{W^{1}}h\psi_{1}}{\int_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}\geq\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle W^{2}\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)}}{{\psi_{2}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W^{2})}}}\frac{\int_{W^{2}}h\psi_{2}}{\int_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}},

which is precisely (
7.3
).

The other conditions are similarly parallel, with (
7.4
) corresponding to the set of functionals in (
7.8
) and (
7.5
) corresponding to the set of functionals in (
7.9
).
∎

We now define the norm
\|h\|
precisely as in (
5.4
), letting
\mathbbm{e}=1
. Then
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
is the completion
of
{\mathcal{A}}
in this norm, It follows that
{\mathcal{S}}\subset{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
.

Finally, following (
5.7
) we define the larger cone
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\supset C_{{\mathbb{R}}}
by

{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}=\{h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\setminus\{0\}:\ell(h)\geq 0,\forall\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}\}.

7.4.
Introduction of Observables and Some Basic Properties

Although we work with sequential maps of the form
f_{k}=T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}
,

T_{j}\in{\mathcal{F}}(Q_{k},E_{*};\varepsilon)
according to Proposition
7.3
,
we would like to prove the Central Limit Theorem for observables sampled at each billiard
collision rather than at each
N_{\mathcal{F}}
collisions. To this end, we will work with observables
of the form

(7.10)

g_{k}=\sum_{j=0}^{N_{{\mathcal{F}}}-1}\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1},

where each
\tilde{g}_{k,j}\in C^{\alpha}(M)
,
26
26

26

We use the norm
|g|_{C^{\alpha}}=\|g\|_{\infty}+\sup_{x,y}\frac{|g(x)-g(y)|}{d(x,y)^{\alpha}}
, so that
C^{\alpha}
is a Banach algebra.
with
\alpha\in(0,1/3)

from the definition of
C_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, and with
|\tilde{g}_{k,j}|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq\tilde{K}
for some
\tilde{K}>0

and all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
j\leq N_{\mathcal{F}}-1
.

For this class of observables, we will prove (O-
1
) in several steps.
The first step is the following Lemma, whose proof is postponed to Section
7.10
.

Lemma 7.5
.

There exists
C_{0}>0
such that for all
g\in C^{\alpha}(M)
and
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
,

\|gh\|\leq C_{0}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
.
In particular, setting
C_{\star}=c+8C_{s}^{q}+4(c+2^{q}\delta^{\gamma}10a)+8\delta^{\gamma}+12C_{s}(2/c)^{\frac{1}{q}-1}
, if

(7.11)

\begin{split}&\lambda>\Xi_{*}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\,\mbox{ with }\\
&\Xi_{*}=\max\left\{\frac{(3+2L)L(1+2a)}{L-1},\frac{(5+2L)A(1+2a)}{A-2^{1-q}},\frac{C_{\star}+2cA(1+2a)L}{cA-2C_{s}}\right\},\end{split}

then
\lambda\pm gh\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and so
\|gh\|\leq\lambda
.

Remark 7.6
.

Note that if
\lambda>0
,
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\lambda-h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
, then necessarily,

\frac{\int_{W}(\lambda-h)\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}>0\quad\implies\quad\lambda>\frac{\int_{W}h\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}\,,

for each
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
. Taking the supremum over
W
and
\psi
, we
conclude that
\|h\|\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}
.

On the other hand, according to Lemma
7.5
with
g\equiv 1
,
\|h\|\leq C_{0}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
. Thus
for
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, the two quantities are equivalent, i.e.

{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\leq\|h\|\leq C_{0}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

Corollary 7.7
.

If
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
g\in C^{\alpha}(M)
, then
gh\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
\|gh\|\leq 3C_{0}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h\|
, where
C_{0}>0
from Lemma
7.5
is independent of
g
and
h
.

Proof.

For
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
,
h\neq 0
, by (
5.4
),
h+\|h\|,\|h\|\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\cup\{0\}

(recalling that in the present setting
\mathbbm{e}=1
). Then writing

gh=g(h+\|h\|)-g\|h\|
implies

\|gh\|\leq\|g(h+\|h\|)\|+\|g\|h\|\,\|\leq C_{0}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h+\|h\|\,\|+C_{0}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h\|

where we have used the fact that
\|1\|=1
and applied Lemma
7.5
twice.
∎

Remark 7.8
.

Theorem
7.2
provides a bound on the norm only for sequences of operators of length
N_{{\mathcal{F}}}
. To obtain finer information (for example, a CLT for observables sampled at each
billiard collision rather than sampled once every
N_{{\mathcal{F}}}
collisions) requires us to estimate uniformly the norms of the operators
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
for each
T\in{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
. Note that this does not follow from
[
8
]
. In fact, the estimates in
[
8
]
cannot even be used to prove that
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\in L({\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbb{R}})
.

The issue pointed out in Remark
7.8
is solved by the next Proposition proven in Section
7.11
.

Proposition 7.9
.

There exists
C_{L}>0
such that for all
T\in{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
and all
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
,

\|{\mathcal{L}}_{T}h\|\leq C_{L}\|h\|
.

7.5.
Verification of Hypothesis (O-
1
)

To verify (O-
1
), we begin with
j=1
.
We must bound the norm of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}h)
for
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
.
Since
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}={\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}
, we have

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}h)=\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\cdot h)=\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j+1}}(\tilde{g}_{k,j}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}h).

Using the triangle inequality and applying Proposition
7.9

N_{{\mathcal{F}}}-j
times to the
j
th term yields,

(7.12)

\begin{split}\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}h)\|&\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-j}\|\tilde{g}_{k,j}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}h\|\\
&\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}3C_{0}|\tilde{g}_{k,j}|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h\|\leq 3N_{{\mathcal{F}}}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}C_{0}\tilde{K}\|h\|\,,\end{split}

where we have applied Corollary
7.7
followed by Proposition
7.9
again in the second line.
This proves (O-
1
) for
j=1
with
K=3N_{{\mathcal{F}}}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}C_{0}\tilde{K}
.

For
j=2
, we proceed similarly. Now,

\begin{split}g_{k}^{2}&=\sum_{i,j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\cdot\tilde{g}_{k,i}\circ T_{i}\circ\cdots T_{1}\\
&=\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\tilde{g}_{k,j}^{2}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}+2\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\cdot\tilde{g}_{k,i}\circ T_{i}\circ\cdots T_{1}\,.\end{split}

Multiplying by
h
and applying
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}={\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}
yields,

\begin{split}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}h)&=\sum_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j+1}}(\tilde{g}_{k,j}^{2}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}h)\\
&\quad+2\sum_{j=1}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j+1}}(\tilde{g}_{k,j}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{i+1}}(\tilde{g}_{k,i}{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{i}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}h))\,.\end{split}

And applying Lemma
7.5
and Proposition
7.9
and Corollary
7.7
as in
(
7.12
) yields,

\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}^{2}h)\|\leq N_{\mathcal{F}}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}3C_{0}\tilde{K}^{2}\|h\|+N_{{\mathcal{F}}}(N_{\mathcal{F}}-1)C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}9C_{0}^{2}\tilde{K}^{2}\|h\|\leq 9C_{0}^{2}N_{\mathcal{F}}^{2}\tilde{K}^{2}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}\|h\|,

where we have used the fact that
|\tilde{g}_{k,j}^{2}|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq|\tilde{g}_{k,j}|_{C^{\alpha}}^{2}
.
This proves (O-
1
) for
j=2
since the constant is
\leq K^{2}
.

For
j=3
the process is similar, using the fact that

g_{k}^{3}=\sum_{i,j,\ell=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\tilde{g}_{k,\ell}\circ T_{\ell}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\cdot\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\cdot\tilde{g}_{k,i}\circ T_{i}\circ\cdots T_{1}\,.

Then estimating precisely as in the case
j=2
, we obtain,

\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(g_{k}^{3}h)\|\leq N_{\mathcal{F}}^{3}27C_{0}^{3}\tilde{K}^{3}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}\|h\|\,,

which is the required estimate for
j=3
. The same strategy works for any
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, completing the verification
of (O-
1
) with
j_{0}=\infty
.

7.6.
Contraction of the Complex Cone
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

With
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
defined as in Section
7.3
, we proceed to define
their complexifications
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
precisely as in Section
5
,
specifically Definition
5.3
.

In light of Remark
7.6
, we define

(7.13)

\mathbbm{m}(h):=\int_{M}h\,d\mu_{\mbox{\tiny SRB}}\qquad\mbox{for $h\in C_{{\mathbb{R}}}$.}

By
[
8
, Remark 7.2]
, there exists
\bar{C}\geq 1
such that

(7.14)

\bar{C}^{-1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq\mathbbm{m}(h)\leq\bar{C}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+},

and since
{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\cdot{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
is equivalent
to
\|\cdot\|
by Remark
7.6
,
\mathbbm{m}
extends to a linear functional on
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and thus to
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
. Indeed, disintegrating
\mu_{\mbox{\tiny SRB}}
as in the proof of

[
8
, Lemma 7.1]
it follows that
\mathbbm{m}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{*}
, where, as in Section
5

(see definition (
5.6
)),
{\mathcal{S}}_{*}
denotes the weak-* closure of the convex hull of

\{\lambda\ell:\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+},\ell\in{\mathcal{S}}\}
.
Now
\mathbbm{m}(1)=1
and combining (
7.14
) with Remark
7.6

implies that
\mathbbm{m}
satisfies
(
5.6
) and therefore (
5.11
) with
\kappa=C_{0}^{-1}\bar{C}^{-1}
.

For fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
j\leq k\leq n
,
\lambda>0
, define the complex operators

{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}
and their concatenations
as in (
3.7
),
27
27

27

Recall that
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}={\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}
.

{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(e^{i\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k}}h),\quad{\mathcal{L}}_{k,j,\lambda}={\mathcal{L}}_{k}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(e^{i\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}\sum_{m=j}^{k}g_{m}\circ f_{m,j}}h)\,.

The main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 7.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
and
f_{k}=T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}
be as in Proposition
7.3
.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
be as defined in (
7.3
)-(
7.5
) and Lemma
7.4
,
and let
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
be its complexification as in Definition
5.3
with
associated projective metric
\delta_{{\mathcal{C}}}
as in (
5.14
).

There exists
\lambda_{0}>0
such that for all
k,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq n
, and
all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|\lambda|\sigma_{n}^{-1}<\lambda_{0}
,

{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}({\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
and
\operatorname{diam}_{\delta_{{\mathcal{C}}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}({\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}))=:\Delta_{{\mathbb{C}}}<\infty
.

Proof.

We will apply Theorem
5.17
to the operators
{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}
.
First, by Proposition
7.3
, the real transfer operators
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
satisfy

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\in L({\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})
and
\operatorname{diam}_{H}({\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})):=\Delta_{{\mathbb{R}}}<\infty
.
Moreover, Lemma
7.5
with
g\equiv 1
implies that
\mathbbm{e}=1
satisfies
(
5.3
), while (
7.14
) and Remark
7.6

imply that
\mathbbm{m}(\cdot)=\mu_{\mbox{\tiny SRB}}(\cdot)
satisfies (
5.6
).

Next, for
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
,

(7.15)

{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}h={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(e^{i\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k}}h)={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\cos(\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k})h)+i{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\sin(\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k})h)\,.

Then temporarily labelling
t=\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}
for brevity, we can write,

\cos(t(\tilde{g}_{k,1}\circ T_{1}+\tilde{g}_{k,0}))=\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,1})\circ T_{1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})+\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,1})\circ T_{1}\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})\,,

so that inductively, using Corollary
7.7
and estimating as in (
7.12
),
it follows that
{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}
defines a bounded operator on
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

by Lemma
2.5
.

It remains to verify condition (
5.23
) of Theorem
5.17
. We will do this via
Lemma
5.18
, which implies (
5.23
). Recalling
(
7.15
), Lemma
5.18
with
a(h)={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\cos(\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k})h)
and

b(h)={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\sin(\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}g_{k})h)

follows immediately from the following lemma.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
g_{k}
be as in (
7.10
).
For all
\varepsilon>0
there exists
t_{0}>0
such that for all
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, all
f_{k}
and all
t\in(-t_{0},t_{0})
,

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{L}}_{k}[\varepsilon h\pm 2h(1-\cos(tg_{k}))]\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,,\\
&{\mathcal{L}}_{k}[\varepsilon h\pm 2h\sin(tg_{k})]\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,.\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
7.11
.

Let
\psi_{t}=2(1-\cos(tg_{k}))
and let
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
.
First we claim that

(7.16)

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)\|\leq C|t|\|h\|\,,

for some
C>0
independent of
t
,
k
and
h
.

To prove the claim, expand
\cos(tg_{k})
using (
7.10
) to obtain,

(7.17)

\cos(tg_{k})=\prod_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})+P_{k}(t)\,,

where
P_{k}(t)
is a polynomial of degree
N_{\mathcal{F}}
in terms involving
\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j})
and

\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j})
. Indeed, every term in
P_{k}(t)
has at least one factor of the form

\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})
.

First we estimate the leading term of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)
,

(7.18)

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\Big(\Big(1-\prod_{j=0}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})\Big)h\Big)={\mathcal{L}}_{k}\big(\big(1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})\big)h\big)\\
&+\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\Big(\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})-\prod_{j=0}^{\ell}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})\Big)h\Big).\end{split}

By Lemma
7.5
and Proposition (
7.9
),

\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\big(\big(1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})\big)h\big)\|\leq C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}C_{0}|1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h\|\leq C^{\prime}|t|\|h\|\,.

Next, for each
\ell
, using that
{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{j}}1=1
,

(7.19)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&\Big(\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})-\prod_{j=0}^{\ell}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})\Big)h\Big)\\
&={\mathcal{L}}_{k}\Big(\big(1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell}\circ T_{\ell}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1}\big)\prod_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})h\Big)\\
&={\mathcal{L}}_{T_{N_{\mathcal{F}}}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{\ell+1}}\Big(\big(1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell})\big){\mathcal{L}}_{T_{\ell}}\big(\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell-1})\\
&\phantom{=\;}\times{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{\ell-1}}\big(\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell-2})\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}\big(\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})h\big)\big)\big)\Big).\end{split}

Again using Lemma
7.5
and Proposition (
7.9
), the norm of this expression is bounded by

\begin{split}C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}-\ell-1}&C_{0}|1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell})|_{C^{\alpha}}\left\|{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{\ell}}\left(\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell-1}){\mathcal{L}}_{T_{\ell-1}}\big(\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{T_{1}}\big(\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,0})h\big)\big)\right)\right\|\\
&\leq C_{L}^{N_{\mathcal{F}}}C_{0}^{\ell+1}\tilde{K}^{\ell}|1-\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,\ell})|_{C^{\alpha}}\|h\|\leq C^{\prime\prime}|t|\|h\|\,.\end{split}

Combining these estimates in (
7.18
) and using the triangle inequality proves the required bound
for the leading term of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)
from (
7.17
). According to (
7.17
),
the remaining terms of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)
are given by
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(P_{k}(t)h)
. These terms can be estimated
in a similar manner as the product of cosines.
Since each term of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(P_{k}(t)h)
contains one factor of the form

\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j})
and
|\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j})|_{C^{\alpha}}=\mathcal{O}(|t|)
, the claim (
7.16
) is proved.

Since

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, there exists
E_{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
with

E_{t}\leq C|t|{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
such that

E_{t}\pm{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\psi_{t}h)\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
. Then,

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\varepsilon h\pm h\psi_{t})={\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\varepsilon h-E_{t})+E_{t}\pm{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(h\psi_{t})=\varepsilon({\mathcal{L}}_{k}h-\varepsilon^{-1}E_{t})+E_{t}\pm{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(h\psi_{t})\,,

where we have used that
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}1=1
. The second term is an element of
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
by choice of
E_{t}
.

For the first term, we use a stronger fact: that
\operatorname{diam}_{H}({\mathcal{L}}_{k}({\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}))\leq\Delta_{{\mathbb{R}}}<\infty
.
In fact, by
[
8
, Prop. 6.13]
for
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
, we have

\alpha(1,{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h)\geq\frac{(1-\chi)A}{A+1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
, where
A
is the cone parameter from (
7.4
)
and
\chi<1
is the contraction
in cone parameters.

It follows that

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h-\frac{E_{t}}{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\quad\mbox{if}\quad\frac{E_{t}}{\varepsilon}<\alpha(1,{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h),\quad\mbox{which is implied by}\quad\frac{E_{t}}{\varepsilon}<\frac{(1-\chi)A}{A+1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

By choice of
E_{t}
and
[
8
, Lemma 5.4]
,
E_{t}\leq C|t|{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq 2C|t|{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
,
so the above condition is satisfied if

|t|<\varepsilon\frac{(1-\chi)A}{2C(A+1)}\,.

Taking this as our value of
t_{0}
proves the first statement of the lemma.

Similarly, setting
\tilde{\psi}_{t}=2\sin(tg_{k})
, we can expand
2\sin(tg_{k})=\tilde{P}_{k}(t)
where
\tilde{P}_{k}(t)

is a polynomial in
\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})
and
\cos(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})
and each term of
\tilde{P}_{k}(t)
has a factor of the form
\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j}\circ T_{j}\circ\cdots\circ T_{1})
.
Then estimating as in (
7.19
), and using the fact that
|\sin(t\tilde{g}_{k,j})|_{C^{\alpha}}=\mathcal{O}(|t|)
,
we conclude,

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\tilde{\psi}_{t}h)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(\tilde{\psi}_{t}h)\|\leq\tilde{C}|t|\|h\|\,,

for some
\tilde{C}>0
independent of
t
,
k
and
h
. From this point, the same proof with
\tilde{\psi}_{t}
replacing

\psi_{t}
yields the second statement of the lemma.
∎

Fixing
\varepsilon<\frac{\kappa^{2}}{48\sqrt{2}}e^{-2\Delta_{{\mathbb{R}}}}
, let
t_{0}>0
be from Lemma
7.11
.
Set
\lambda_{0}=t_{0}
. Then for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that

|\lambda|\sigma_{n}^{-1}<\lambda_{0}
, setting
t=\lambda\sigma_{n}^{-1}
, we have
|t|<t_{0}
so that Lemma
7.11
implies that
Lemma
5.18
and therefore (
5.23
) holds for
{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda}
.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

7.7.
Verification of (O-
2
) and (O-
3
)

Throughout this section, we assume
|\lambda|<\lambda_{0}\sigma_{n}
, where
\lambda_{0}
is from Theorem
7.10
.
From our choices of
\mathbbm{e}_{k}=1
,
\mathbbm{m}_{k}(h)=\int_{M}h\,d\mu_{\mbox{\tiny SRB}}
, property (a)
of Section
5.3
holds with
\bar{\kappa}=\kappa=C_{0}^{-1}\bar{C}^{-1}
from
Section
7.6
. Moreover, property (b) of Section
5.3
is trivially
satisfied with
K=1
, and property (c)
follows from Theorem
7.10
with
{\mathbb{L}}_{k}={\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda,n}
.

Hence setting
\alpha_{k,j,\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{k,j}
,
h_{k,j,\lambda}=\boldsymbol{h}_{k,j}
and
\ell_{k,j,\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\ell}_{k,j}
,
Lemma
5.19
implies

\|h_{k,j,\lambda}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\kappa},\quad\|\ell_{k,j,\lambda}\|^{\prime}\leq\sqrt{2}

while
|\alpha_{k,j,\lambda}|\leq 1
, by the invariance of the
SRB
measure. This implies,

\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k,j,\lambda}h\|\leq\frac{2}{\kappa}\|h\|,

which verifies (O-
2
) with
K=2/\kappa
.

Condition (O-
3
) is verified exactly as in Section
6.4

since Theorem
7.10
implies that Theorem
5.15
applies to the
operators
{\mathcal{L}}_{k,\lambda,n}
.

7.8.
Central Limit Theorem for Sequential Dispersing Billiards

We collect our previous results on the contraction of the relevant operators in order to state the culminating
Central Limit Theorem for sequential dispersing billiards. This is essentially a restatement of
Theorem
2.2
, but now all the relevant objects have been properly defined.

Theorem 7.12
.

Fix
\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*}>0
and let
{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
be the associated family of billiard maps. Let

\rho\in C_{{\mathbb{R}}}
,
\int\rho d\mu_{\mbox{\tiny SRB}}=1
.
For a sequence of observables
(\tilde{g}_{k,j})
as defined in (
7.10
), choose an
N_{\mathcal{F}}
-admissible sequence

(T_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
as defined in Definition
7.1
, and define the centered observables

\hat{g}_{k}
as in (
2.8
). Then, recalling the distribution function
F_{n}(x)
from (
2.16
),
for all
c_{\star}\in(0,1)
there
exists
\bar{C}>0
such that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sigma_{n}\geq\max\{1,c_{\star}n^{1/3}\ln(n+1)\}
, we have

\left|F_{n}(x)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{-\frac{y^{2}}{2}}dy\right|\leq\bar{C}\sigma_{n}^{-3}(\ln\sigma_{n})^{2}n.

Proof.

We have verified hypotheses (C-
1
)-(C-
4
) in Section
7.2
and
conditions (O-
1
)-(O-
3
) in Section
7.7
, accordingly
the theorem is an imediate application of Theorem
2.7
and Corollary
2.9
.
∎

The remaining issue is to verify the condition of the growth of the variance.

We start with the following general estimate

(7.20)

\begin{split}&\sigma_{n}^{2}:=\int[\hat{S}_{n}](x)^{2}\rho(x)dx\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}h_{k}+2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n-1}\int\hat{g}_{j}{\mathcal{L}}_{j-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}h_{k}\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}h_{k}+2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int\hat{g}_{j+k}{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}h_{k}+{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\nu^{j-k}\right)\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left[\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}h_{k}+2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int\hat{g}_{j+k}{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}h_{k}\right]+{\mathcal{O}}(1),\end{split}

where we have used hypothesis (O-3), with
\nu=e^{-c}
.
In the case of billiards we have
h_{k}=1
. Also, for simplicity, we choose
\rho=1
, and
g_{k}
such that
\int g_{k}=0
, thus
g_{k}=\hat{g}_{k}
.

Lemma 7.13
.

If there exists
C_{0}>0
such
C_{0}\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}\geq\|g_{k}\|_{C^{\alpha}}^{2}
, then, for
\nu
small enough, we have
\sigma_{n}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}

Proof.

We use condition (O-
1
) and (
2.7
) (recall that we have set
e^{-c}=\nu
) to compute

\begin{split}&\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int\hat{g}_{j+k}{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}=\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k}\hat{g}_{j+k}{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}\\
&\geq\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}-2K\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|{\mathcal{L}}_{j+k-1}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\hat{g}_{k}\|_{j+k}\geq\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}-\frac{6K\nu}{1-\nu}\|g_{k}\|_{C^{\alpha}}^{2}\\
&\geq\left[1-\frac{6KC_{0}}{1-\nu}\nu\right]\int\hat{g}_{k}^{2}.\end{split}

The formula (
7.20
) implies the Lemma.
∎

The above Lemma, although effective, is not very satisfactory as it is not easy to check and applies only if
\nu
is small. It would be nice to have the equivalent of Proposition
6.1
; unfortunately, it is not clear how to proceed. It seems natural to introduce a stable foliation, as in
[
29
]
, but there are some technical problems that require extra ideas.

In the literature, there are some relevant results only in the random case
[
11
,
12
,
13
]
, described in the next section. For completeness, we show that the random case can be treated also in our setting.

7.9.
Variance Growth in the Random Case

Let
\Omega={\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbb{N}}
be a probability space with a measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}
. Assume that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}
is invariant and ergodic with respect to the shift.

Let
\{f_{a}\}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}
be a set of piecewise smooth area preserving diffeomorphisms and
\{g_{a}\}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{M}},{\mathbb{R}})
a set of observables.
And suppose that, for each
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{L}}_{a}1=1\\
&\int_{M}g_{a}=0.\end{split}

Let
\mathbbm{g}(\omega,x)=g_{\omega_{0}}(x)
for each
(\omega,x)\in\Omega\times M
and consider the dynamics
F:\Omega\times M\to\Omega\times M
defined by

(7.21)

F(\omega,x)=(\tau\omega,f_{\omega_{0}}(x)).

Then, for each
\omega\in\Omega
, we have the sequential observables

\{\mathbbm{g}\circ F^{k}\}=\{g_{\omega_{k}}\circ f_{\omega_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ f_{\omega_{0}}\},

hence, for a given
\omega\in\Omega
, we can write

\begin{split}&\hat{S}_{n}(\omega,x)=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}g_{\omega_{k}}\circ f_{\omega_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ f_{\omega_{0}}(x)=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\mathbbm{g}\circ F^{k}(\omega,x)\\
&\sigma_{n}^{2}(\omega)=\int_{M}\hat{S}_{n}(\omega,x)^{2}dx.\end{split}

The reader can check
[
11
,
12
,
13
]
and references therein for a discussion of a similar model (but limited to expanding maps) and
[
2
]
(for the case of toral automorphisms) using a related approach. The following result is a generalization of
[
2
]
; similar ideas can be applied to the case of expanding maps.

Theorem 7.14
.

{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}
-a.s. the variance
\sigma_{n}
either grows linearly or there exists
\hat{\psi}\in L^{2}(M)
such that
g_{a}=\hat{\psi}-\hat{\psi}\circ f_{a}
for all
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
, and hence
\sigma_{n}
is uniformly bounded.

Proof.

Let
\beta:\Omega\to{\mathbb{R}}
be defined by

\beta(\omega)=\int_{M}g_{\omega_{0}}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{M}g_{(\tau^{j}\omega)_{0}}{\mathcal{L}}_{(\tau^{j-1}\omega)_{0}}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega_{0}}g_{\omega_{0}},

where
\tau
is the shift. Then by (
7.20
), applied to the sequence determined by
\omega\in\Omega
,

(7.22)

\sigma_{n}^{2}(\omega)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\beta(\tau^{k}\omega)+{\mathcal{O}}(1).

Lemma 7.15
.

Either
\sigma_{n}^{2}
grows linearly
{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}
-a.s. or
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\sigma_{n}^{2})={\mathcal{O}}(1)
.

Proof.

Integrating (
7.22
) yields
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\sigma_{n}^{2})=n{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\beta)+{\mathcal{O}}(1)
.
It follows, by Birkhoff’s ergodic theorem, that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}
almost surely

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sigma_{n}^{2}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\beta).

Thus,
\sigma_{n}^{2}
can grow sublinearly only if
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\beta)=0
, but then
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\sigma_{n}^{2})={\mathcal{O}}(1)
.
∎

Thus, a sublinear growth can happen only if
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\!e}}(\sigma_{n}^{2})={\mathcal{O}}(1)
.
If so, consider the annealed measure
{\mathbb{P}}:={\mathbb{P}}_{e}\times\rho dx
and the related expectation
{\mathbb{E}}
.

The sequence
\hat{S}_{n}
is then uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\Omega\times M,{\mathbb{P}})
, hence it is weakly-compact. Let
\psi\in L^{2}(\Omega\times M,{\mathbb{P}})
be an accumulation point.
Then, for each
\varphi\in L^{2}(\Omega\times M,{\mathbb{P}})
, we have

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi\psi)&=\lim_{j\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi\sum_{k=0}^{n_{j}-1}g_{\omega_{k}}\circ f_{\omega_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ f_{\omega_{0}})\\
&=\lim_{j\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\omega_{0}}\varphi\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}-1}g_{\omega_{k}}\circ f_{\omega_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ f_{\omega_{1}}\right)+{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi g_{\omega_{0}})\\
&={\mathbb{E}}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\omega_{0}}\varphi\cdot\psi\circ\tau\right)+{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi g_{\omega_{0}})={\mathbb{E}}(\varphi\psi\circ F)+{\mathbb{E}}(\varphi g_{\omega_{0}}).\end{split}

It follows that
g_{\omega_{0}}(x)=\psi(\omega,x)-\psi\circ F(\omega,x)
.

Next, let us define
\hat{\psi}(x)={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{e}}(\psi)
,
\bar{g}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{e}}(\mathbbm{g})
and note that

\bar{g}(x)=\hat{\psi}-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{e}}(\hat{\psi}\circ f_{\omega_{0}}).

Furthermore, define

\mathbbm{g}\circ F^{k}=g_{\omega_{k}}\circ f_{\omega_{k-1}}\circ\cdots\circ f_{\omega_{0}}(x)=\hat{\psi}\circ F^{k}-\hat{\psi}\circ F^{k+1}+Z_{k}.

Note that

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{e}}(Z_{k}\;|\;\omega_{0},\dots,\omega_{k})=Z_{k}\\
&{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{e}}(Z_{k}\;|\;\omega_{0},\dots,\omega_{k-1})=0,\end{split}

Hence,
M_{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}Z_{k}
is a reverse martingale. It follows that

{\mathbb{E}}(\hat{S}_{n}^{2})={\mathbb{E}}\left([\hat{\psi}\circ F^{n}-\hat{\psi}+M_{n}]^{2}\right)\geq{\mathbb{E}}(M_{n}^{2})-2{\mathbb{E}}([\hat{\psi}\circ F^{n}-\hat{\psi}]^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}(M_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

Since
\psi\in L^{2}
it follows that there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

C\geq{\mathbb{E}}(M_{n}^{2})=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}{\mathbb{E}}(Z_{k}^{2})=n{\mathbb{E}}(Z_{0}^{2}).

Thus, it must be that

0={\mathbb{E}}(Z_{0}^{2})={\mathbb{E}}([\mathbbm{g}+\hat{\psi}\circ F-\hat{\psi}]^{2}).

That is,
g_{a}+\hat{\psi}-\hat{\psi}\circ f_{a}=0
for each
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Remark 7.16
.

Note that if the
f_{a}
are smooth Anosov maps and the observables
g_{a}
are smooth, then by Livšic’s theory, see
[
5
]
, we have that
\hat{\psi}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{0}
. Hence, the
g_{a}
must be continuous coboundaries, a condition that is checkable. On the contrary, it is not obvious how to check that the
g_{a}
are not
L^{2}
coboundaries, so the application to billiards is still not totally satisfactory. Nevertheless, our result is much sharper than the usual one, e.g. see
[
23
, Equation (2.10)]
where one has an
L^{2}
coboundary only with respect to the map (
7.21
). A similar result is proven in
[
2
]
, but limited to Anosov automorphisms.

7.10.
Proof of Lemma
7.5

To estimate
\|gh\|
, we will find
\lambda>0
such that
\lambda\pm gh\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
. To this end, it will be convenient
to estimate the quantity
\frac{\int_{W}hg\psi\,dm_{W}}{\int_{W}\psi\,dm_{W}}
from above and below, where

W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
. For brevity, we will omit
dm_{W}
when writing the integral.

For
g\in C^{\alpha}(M)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
, we choose

B>0
so that
g\psi+B\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
.
Letting
H^{\beta}(g\psi)
denote the Hölder constant of
g\psi
with exponent
\beta
,
let
B>a^{-1}H^{\beta}(g\psi)+|g\psi|_{\infty}
. It follows that

\frac{g(x)\psi(x)+B}{g(y)\psi(y)+B}\leq\frac{|g(x)\psi(x)-g(y)\psi(y)|}{g(y)\psi(y)+B}+1\leq e^{ad(x,y)^{\beta}}

for all
x,y\in M
, so that in particular,
g\psi+B\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
.
Note that
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
implies, for
x,y\in W
,

(7.23)

\left|\frac{\psi(x)}{\psi(y)}-1\right|\leq e^{ad(x,y)^{\beta}}-1\leq ad(x,y)^{\beta}e^{a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\leq 2ad(x,y)^{\beta}\,,

so that
H^{\beta}(\psi)\leq 2a|\psi|_{C^{0}}
, where we have used
e^{a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\leq 2

by
[
8
, eq. (4.9)]
.
Since
a>1
, we may choose

(7.24)

B\leq|g|_{C^{\beta}}|\psi|_{C^{\beta}}\leq|g|_{C^{\alpha}}(1+2a)|\psi|_{C^{0}}\,.

Now, since
|\psi|_{C^{0}}\leq 2\psi
,

(7.25)

\begin{split}\frac{\int_{W}hg\,\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}&=\frac{\int_{W}h(g\psi+B)}{\int_{W}\psi}-B\frac{\int_{W}h}{\int_{W}\psi}\\
&\leq\frac{\int_{W}h(g\psi+B)}{\int_{W}g\psi+B}\frac{\int_{W}g\psi+B}{\int_{W}\psi}\\
&\leq 3(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\leq 3(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\end{split}

where we have dropped the second term since
{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}>0
for
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Similarly, we estimate the lower bound, dropping the first term, which is positive,

(7.26)

\begin{split}\frac{\int_{W}hg\,\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}&=\frac{\int_{W}h(g\psi+B)}{\int_{W}\psi}-\frac{\int_{W}hB}{\int_{W}\psi}\\
&\geq-2(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\geq-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.\end{split}

Next we prove that if
\lambda
satisfies (
7.11
), then
\lambda+gh\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
In order for
\lambda+gh
to satisfy the first cone condition (
7.3
), we need

\sup_{W,\psi}\frac{\int_{W}(\lambda+gh)\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}\leq L\inf_{W,\psi}\frac{\int_{W}(\lambda+gh)\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}\,.

This is equivalent to

\lambda+\sup_{W,\psi}\frac{\int_{W}gh\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}\leq L\Big(\lambda+\inf_{W,\psi}\frac{\int_{W}gh\psi}{\int_{W}\psi}\Big)\,,

which according to (
7.25
) and (
7.26
) is satisfied if

\lambda+3(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq L(\lambda-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

Thus it suffices to choose
\lambda
so that

(7.27)

\lambda\geq\frac{3+2L}{L-1}L(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

In order to verify the second cone condition (
7.4
),
\lambda>0
should satisfy,

|W|^{-q}\frac{|\int_{W}(\lambda+gh)\psi|}{\fint_{W}\psi}\leq A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+gh{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,

for all
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
,
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
.

The left hand side is bounded above by

(7.28)

\begin{split}&|W|^{1-q}\lambda+|W|^{-q}\frac{|\int_{W}h(g\psi+B)|}{\fint_{W}g\psi+B}\frac{\int_{W}g\psi+B}{\int_{W}\psi}+|W|^{-q}\frac{|\int_{W}hB|}{\fint_{W}\psi}\\
&\leq 2^{1-q}\delta^{1-q}\lambda+5(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\end{split}

while the right hand side is bounded below using (
7.26
),

A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+gh{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\geq A\delta^{1-q}(\lambda-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

Putting these estimates together, it suffices to choose
\lambda>0
so that

2^{1-q}\delta^{1-q}\lambda+5(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq A\delta^{1-q}(\lambda-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})

This holds when

(7.29)

\lambda\geq\frac{(5+2L)A}{A-2^{1-q}}(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

Finally, we find
\lambda
to satisfy the third cone condition (
7.5
),

(7.30)

\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}cA{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+gh{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,

for
W^{1},W^{2}\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
with
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\leq\delta
and

\psi_{i}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\alpha}(W^{i})
satisfying
d_{*}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})=0
.

Without loss of generality, we may assume
|W^{2}|\geq|W^{1}|
and
\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}=1
.
Also, we assume

(7.31)

|W^{2}|^{q}\geq\delta^{q-\gamma}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\tfrac{c}{2}\,,

otherwise, applying the second cone condition to
\lambda+gh
with
\lambda
satisfying (
7.29
) yields,

(7.32)

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|&\leq(|W^{1}|^{q}+|W^{2}|^{q})A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+gh{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\hskip-14.22636pt\leq 2A\delta^{1-\gamma}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\tfrac{c}{2}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+gh{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\end{split}

which is precisely the needed estimate with no additional condition on
\lambda
.

We proceed to estimate both sides of (
7.30
) under the assumption (
7.31
).
As before, the right-hand side of (
7.30
) is bounded below by,

(7.33)

d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}cA(\lambda-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

To bound the left-hand side of (
7.30
) from above,
we first split up the differences, using that
\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}=1
,

(7.34)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\,\lambda||W^{1}|-W^{2}||+\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}hg\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\\
&\leq\lambda||W^{1}|-W^{2}||+\frac{\left|\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\left|\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}-1\right|+\left|\int_{W^{1}}hg\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\,.\end{split}

The first term above is bounded by
[
8
, eq. (5.8)]
,

(7.35)

\left||W^{1}|-|W^{2}|\right|\leq 2C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\,,

where
C_{s}=\sqrt{1+({\mathcal{K}}_{*}^{-1}+\tau_{*}^{-1})^{2}}
denotes the maximum absolute value of the slope of stable curves in
{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
,
while the difference in the second term above is bounded by
[
8
, eq. (5.10)]
,

(7.36)

\left|\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}-1\right|=|W^{2}|^{-1}\left|\int_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}-|W^{2}|\right|\leq\frac{6C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})}{|W^{2}|}\,.

Moreover, by (
7.28
),

\frac{\left|\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\leq 5(1+2a)|W^{2}|^{q}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

Combining these estimates in (
7.34
) yields the following upper bound for the
left side of (
7.30
)

(7.37)

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq\lambda 2C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\\
&+\frac{\delta^{1-q}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})}{|W^{2}|^{1-q}}5(1+2a)6C_{s}A|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}+\left|\int_{W^{1}}hg\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\\
&\leq\lambda 2C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})+4C_{s}^{q}(1+2a)A\delta^{1-\gamma}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\;+\left|\int_{W^{1}}hg\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\,,\end{split}

where we have used (
7.31
) together with
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\leq\delta
,

\gamma\leq q<1/2
and the constraint
c\geq 16C_{s}^{q}
from
[
8
, eq. (5.7)]
.

To estimate the last term in (
7.37
),
we recall the notation of matched and unmatched pieces.
Recall that each curve
W^{i}
is expressed as the graph of a function over an arclength interval
I_{i}
,

W^{i}=\{G_{i}(r)=(r,\varphi_{i}(r):r\in I_{i}\}

Since
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{1})\leq\delta
, we have
|I_{1}\cap I_{2}|>0
.
Let

U^{i}=\{G_{i}(r):r\in I_{1}\cap I_{2}\}
denote the matched pieces and let
V^{i}
denote the at most two unmatched pieces.
Remark that
|V^{i}|\leq C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})
.
Choose
B>0
such that
g\psi_{2}+B\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\frac{a}{2},\alpha}(U^{2})
. Following (
7.24
)
we may choose
B\leq 2(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}|\psi_{2}|_{C^{0}}
.
Define the following functions on
U^{1}
,

\begin{split}\tilde{g}&=g\circ G_{2}\circ G_{1}^{-1}\cdot\frac{\|G^{\prime}_{2}\|}{\|G^{\prime}_{1}\|}\circ G_{1}^{-1}\,;\quad\tilde{\psi}_{2}=\psi\circ G_{2}\circ G_{1}^{-1}\\
\tilde{B}&=B\cdot\frac{\|G^{\prime}_{2}\|}{\|G^{\prime}_{1}\|}\circ G_{1}^{-1}\,.\end{split}

Then by definition,
d_{*}(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B},g\psi_{2}+B)=0
.
Moreover,
in the proof of
[
8
, Lemma 5.5(c)]
, it is shown that for
r,s\in I_{1}\cap I_{2}
,
x=G_{k}(r)
,

y=G_{k}(s)
,

(7.38)

\frac{\|G^{\prime}_{k}(r)\|}{\|G^{\prime}_{k}(s)\|}\leq e^{B_{*}d(x,y)}\,\,\mbox{ and }\,\,\frac{\|G^{\prime}_{2}(r)\|}{\|G^{\prime}_{1}(r)\|}\leq e^{d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(U^{1},U^{2})}\leq e^{\delta}\leq 2\,,

for some constant
B_{*}
depending only on the maximum curvature of curves in

{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
. This implies in particular that

\tilde{B}\leq 2B
.
and that
\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\alpha}(U^{2})

by
[
8
, eq. (5.31)]
, as long as
2B_{*}(2\delta)^{1-\alpha}\leq\frac{a}{2}
, which
is compatible with the restriction on
\delta
from
[
8
, eq. (5.32)]
.

With these preparations, we are ready to estimate the difference of integrals in (
7.37
)
by splitting into matched and unmatched pieces,

(7.39)

\begin{split}\Big|\int_{W^{1}}&hg\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\Big|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int_{V^{i}}hg\psi_{i}\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}}hg\psi_{1}-\int_{U^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|\int_{V^{i}}hg\psi_{i}\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}-\int_{U^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\,.\end{split}

The following sublemma allows us to estimate the principal differences in (
7.39
).

Sublemma 7.17
.

The following estimates hold true

a)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})\right|\leq 12(3+2a)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}\delta A|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
.

b)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{U^{1}}h\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}-\int_{U^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\leq 20(1+2a)(c+3\delta)Ad_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
.

Postponing the proof of the sublemma, we use it to complete the estimate for the third cone condition.
Using the sublemma, together with (
7.28
) to bound the integrals on unmatched pieces,
and recalling that
|V^{i}|\leq C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})
, we
bound (
7.39
) by

\begin{split}\left|\int_{W^{1}}hg\psi_{1}\right.&-\left.\int_{W^{2}}hg\psi_{2}\right|\leq 40(1+2a)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{q}C_{s}^{q}A\delta^{1-q}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&+12(3+2a)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}\delta A|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&+20(1+2a)(c+3\delta)Ad_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\leq 4(1+2a)(10C_{s}^{q}+9\delta^{\gamma}+5c+15\delta)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}A|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\end{split}

where we have used that
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\leq\delta
and
\gamma\leq\min\{q,\alpha-\beta\}
.
This estimate combined with (
7.37
) yields our final upper bound for the left hand side of
(
7.30
),

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+gh)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq&2\lambda C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}\\
&+C_{\star}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,\end{split}

where
C_{\star}=4A(1+2a)(14C_{s}^{q}+9\delta^{\gamma}+5c+15\delta)
.
Combining this with the lower bound (
7.33
), we see that (
7.30
) will be satisfied
if

\lambda 2C_{s}+C_{\star}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\leq cA(\lambda-2(1+2a)L|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

Since
cA>2C_{s}
by
[
8
, eq. (5.36)]
, this in turn is satisfied if

(7.40)

\lambda\geq\frac{C_{\star}+2cA(1+2a)L}{cA-2C_{s}}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

Now taking together (
7.27
), (
7.29
) and
(
7.40
), we conclude that
\lambda+gh\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
if

\lambda
satisfies (
7.11
).

We claim that the same value of
\lambda
implies that
\lambda-gh\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
. To see this, write

\lambda-gh=\lambda+(-g)h
, and notice that
-g\in C^{\alpha}
with
|-g|_{C^{\alpha}}=|g|_{C^{\alpha}}
.
Applying the previous argument to
-gh
, we see that
\lambda+(-g)h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathbb{R}}
when

\lambda
satisfies (
7.11
). This completes the proof of the lemma.

It remains to prove the sublemma used during the argument.

Proof of Sublemma
7.17
.

We prove the two statements one at a time:

a) We would like to apply the second cone
condition (
7.4
) to the integral; however,
g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}
may not be in
{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(U^{1})
. To remedy this, first note that
for
x=G_{1}(r)\in U^{1}
, using the fact that
d_{*}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})=0
,

\begin{split}|g(x)\psi_{1}(x)-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}(x)|&=\tfrac{1}{\|G^{\prime}_{1}\|}|g\psi_{1}(G_{1}(r))\cdot\|G^{\prime}_{1}(r)\|-g\psi_{2}(G_{2}(r))\cdot\|G^{\prime}_{2}\||\\
&\leq|\psi_{1}(G_{1}(r))||g(G_{1}(r))-g(G_{2}(r))|\\
&\leq 2H^{\alpha}(g)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha}\,,\end{split}

where we have used the bound
|\psi_{1}|\leq e^{a(2\delta)^{\alpha}}\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}\leq 2
.

So on the one hand,

|(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(x)-(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(y)|\leq 4H^{\alpha}(g)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha}\,,\mbox{ for }x,y\in U^{1}\,.

On the other hand, for
x=G_{1}(r)\in U^{1}
, let
\tilde{x}=G_{2}(r)\in U^{2}
denote the corresponding point in
U^{2}
. Then,

\begin{split}d(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=&\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\sqrt{1+(d\varphi_{2}/dr)^{2}}\,dr\leq\sup_{I_{1}\cap I_{2}}\sqrt{\frac{1+(d\varphi_{2}/dr)^{2}}{1+(d\varphi_{1}/dr)^{2}}}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\sqrt{1+(d\varphi_{1}/dr)^{2}}\,dr\\
&\leq 2d(x,y)\,,\end{split}

where we have used the estimate before
[
8
, eq. (5.9)]
to estimate the ratio of Jacobians. Then
recalling (
7.24
),

\begin{split}|(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(x)-(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(y)|&\leq|g\psi_{1}(x)-g\psi_{1}(y)|+|g(\tilde{x})\psi_{1}(x)-g(\tilde{y})\psi_{1}(y)|\\
&\leq|g|_{C^{\alpha}}|\psi_{1}|_{C^{\alpha}}(d(x,y)^{\alpha}+d(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})^{\alpha})\\
&\leq 6(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}d(x,y)^{\alpha}\,.\end{split}

Putting these estimates together, we see that

\frac{|(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(x)-(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})(y)|}{d(x,y)^{\beta}}\leq\frac{|g|_{C^{\alpha}}}{d(x,y)^{\beta}}\min\{4d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha},6(1+2a)d(x,y)^{\alpha}\}\,,

and the expression is maximized when the two quantities are equal, i.e. when
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})=(3(1+2a)/2)^{1/\alpha}d(x,y)
. Thus,

H^{\beta}_{U^{1}}(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g})\leq 6(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}\,.

So we choose a constant
\Delta\geq a^{-1}H^{\beta}_{U^{1}}(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})+|g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}|_{C^{0}(U^{1})}
, so that

g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\Delta\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(U^{1})
, and
\Delta\leq|g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}|_{C^{\beta}(U^{1})}\leq(8+6a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}
.

Now applying (
7.4
) completes the proof of statement (a) of the sublemma,

\begin{split}\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2})\right|&\leq\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\Delta)\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h\Delta\right|\\
&\leq|U^{1}|^{q}\fint_{U^{1}}(g\psi_{1}-\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+2\Delta)A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\leq 2(18+12a)d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}\delta A|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.\end{split}

b) With
B
and
\tilde{B}
as chosen before the statement of the sublemma, write,

(7.41)

\begin{split}\left|\int_{U^{1}}\!\!\!h\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}-\int_{U^{2}}\!\!\!hg\psi_{2}\right|&\leq\left|\frac{\int_{U^{1}}h(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B})}{\fint_{U^{1}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B}}-\frac{\int_{U^{2}}h(g\psi_{2}+B)}{\fint_{U^{2}}g\psi_{2}+B}\right|\fint_{U^{2}}\!\!(g\psi_{2}+B)\\
&+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B})\right|\left|1-\frac{|U^{1}|}{|U^{2}|}\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h\tilde{B}-\int_{U^{2}}hB\right|.\end{split}

The first term of (
7.41
) can be estimated using (
7.5
) since

d_{*}(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B},g\psi_{2}+B)=0
and both are valid test functions by choice of
B
. For the second
term of (
7.41
), we use
[
8
, eq. (5.24)]
to estimate

(7.42)

\left|1-\frac{|U^{2}|}{|U^{1}|}\right|\leq d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(U^{1},U^{2})\leq d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\,.

Using this together with (
7.4
) yields the bound,

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\int_{U^{1}}h(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B})}{\fint_{U^{1}}\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B}}-\frac{\int_{U^{2}}h(g\psi_{2}+B)}{\fint_{U^{2}}g\psi_{2}+B}\right|\fint_{U^{2}}(g\psi_{2}+B)+\left|\int_{U^{1}}h(\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B})\right|\left|1-\frac{|U^{1}|}{|U^{2}|}\right|\\
&\leq cAd_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}12(1+2a)|g|_{C^{\alpha}}+36(1+2a)A\delta|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\,.\end{split}

Moreover, it is clear that the final term in (
7.41
) can be estimated in an analogous way, with

B
and
\tilde{B}
replacing the test functions
g\psi_{2}+B
and
\tilde{g}\tilde{\psi}_{2}+\tilde{B}
. Thus,

\begin{split}\left|\int_{U^{1}}h\tilde{B}-\int_{U^{2}}hB\right|&\leq BcAd_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}+3Bd_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\delta A{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\leq 8(1+2a)Ad_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}|g|_{C^{\alpha}}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}(3\delta+c)\,.\end{split}

Putting this estimate together with the previous one completes the proof of statement (b), using that

d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\leq\delta
.
∎

7.11.
Proof of Proposition
7.9
(Bounding the Norm of
{\mathcal{L}}_{T}
)

As mentioned in Remark
7.8
, the goal of this section is to obtain a bound on
\|{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\|
, that is, for the action of a single operator rather than a sequence of
N_{\mathcal{F}}
operators. The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
7.5
in Section
7.11
. Unfortunately, the details are different enough that we need to carry it out explicitely.

We will first bound
{\mathcal{L}}h
for
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and then show how this
extends to all
h\in{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
as in the proof of Corollary
7.7
.

Let
h\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
and
T\in{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
with
{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{L}}_{T}
. We must estimate
the norm of
{\mathcal{L}}h
. To this end, we first obtain bounds on
{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}
and
{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
.
Let
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
. Then,

(7.43)

\int_{W}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi=\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\,\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\,\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T\,,

where we have changed variables and denote by
L_{1}(W)
the connected homogenous components
of
T^{-1}W=\{W_{i}\}_{i}
longer than
\delta
, and by
S_{1}(W)
those homogeneous components shorter than

\delta
.

Following (
7.24
), in order to transform the
\psi\circ TJ_{W_{i}}T
into valid test functions, we choose
B_{i}
such that

(7.44)

\begin{split}a^{-1}H^{\beta}(\psi\circ TJ_{W_{i}}T)+&|\psi\circ TJ_{W_{i}}T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}<B_{i}\leq|J_{W_{i}}T\psi\circ T|_{C^{\beta}(W_{i})}\\
&\leq(1+2aC_{1}+C_{d})|J_{W_{i}}T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}|\psi\circ T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}\,,\end{split}

where
C_{d}>0
is a distortion constant for
J_{W^{i}}T
, uniform for
T\in{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})

as in
[
8
, eq. (3.5)]
, and we have used (
7.23
) to bound the distortion of
\psi\circ T

together with
d(Tx,Ty)\leq C_{1}d(x,y)
, where
C_{1}\geq 1
is the minimum hyperbolicity
constant
28
28

28

That is, setting
\Lambda=1+2{\mathcal{K}}_{*}\tau_{*}
,

C_{1}\geq 1
satisfies
\|DT^{-n}(x)v\|\geq C_{1}^{-1}\Lambda^{n}\|v\|
for
all
T\in{\mathcal{F}}(\tau_{*},{\mathcal{K}}_{*},E_{*})
and
v
in the stable cone.

from
[
8
, eq. (3.1)]
.
Then as in Section
7.10
,
J_{W_{i}}T\psi\circ T+B_{i}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
.
For ease of notation, let us name the combined distortion constants,

C_{a}:=1+2aC_{1}+C_{d}\,.

Now we estimate the contribution from long pieces in (
7.43
) using (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), as well as the upper bound on
B_{i}
from (
7.44
),

(7.45)

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\,&(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-B_{i}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\\
&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+2B_{i})\\
&\leq\big[1+2C_{a}e^{C_{d}(2\delta)^{1/3}+a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\big]L{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{TW_{i}}\psi\\
&\leq\big[1+8C_{a}\big]L{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\int_{W}\psi\,,\end{split}

recalling that by
[
8
, Sect. 5.3]
,
\delta
is small enough that

e^{a(2\delta)^{\beta}}
and
e^{C_{d}(2\delta)^{1/3}}
are each less than 2.

Next we estimate the contribution from short pieces in (
7.43
), using (
7.4
),

(7.46)

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}&\int_{W_{i}}h\,(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-B_{i}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\\
&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}A\delta^{1-q}|W_{i}|^{q}\fint_{W_{i}}(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+2B_{i})\\
&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\delta\big[1+2C_{a})\big]|\psi|_{C^{0}(W)}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}|J_{W_{i}}T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}.\end{split}

Since
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
and
|W|\geq\delta
, we estimate

\delta|\psi|_{C^{0}(W)}\leq\delta e^{a|W|^{\beta}}\fint_{W}\psi\leq 2\int_{W}\psi
, recalling again
that
e^{a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\leq 2
.
The sum over Jacobians is bounded by
C_{0}\theta_{0}
, which is the uniform
one-step expansion estimate stated in
[
8
, eq. (3.4)]
, where
C_{0}\geq 1
and
\theta_{0}<1
.
Putting these bounds together yields,

(7.47)

\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\,\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}\int_{W}\psi\,.

Substituting (
7.45
) and (
7.47
) into (
7.43
)
and taking the supremum over
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
yields,

(7.48)

{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\Big(L\big[1+8C_{a}\big]+A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}\Big)\,.

Next, we need the analogous lower bound for
{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}
. Taking
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}(\delta)

and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
, we first change variables as in (
7.43
).
For the estimate on long pieces, we use the fact that integrals of
h
against valid test functions
on curves of length at least
\delta
are positive by the first cone condition, (
7.3
).
Then, using (
7.44
) and the distortion bounds as before,

(7.49)

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}&\int_{W_{i}}h\,(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-B_{i}\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\\
&\geq\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\int_{W_{i}}(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}\int_{W_{i}}B_{i}\\
&\geq\sum_{i\in L_{1}(W)}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}2\int_{TW_{i}}\psi-L{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}C_{a}e^{C_{d}(2\delta)^{1/3}+a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\int_{TW_{i}}\psi\\
&\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\big[2-4LC_{a}\big]\int_{W}\psi\,.\end{split}

The contribution from short pieces is estimated using (
7.4
) as in (
7.47
),
but with the observation that the integrals of
h
on short pieces can be negative,

(7.50)

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}&\int_{W_{i}}h\,\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-B_{i}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\\
&\geq-{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}A\delta^{1-q}|W_{i}|^{q}\fint_{W_{i}}(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+2B_{i})\\
&\geq-{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}\int_{W}\psi\,.\end{split}

Putting together (
7.49
) and (
7.50
) and taking the appropriate infima yields,

(7.51)

{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\Big(2-4LC_{a}-A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}\Big)\,.

We are now in a position to choose
\lambda>0
so that
\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h
satisfies the
first cone condition (
7.3
). To simplify notation, define

H_{0}=L\big[1+8C_{a}\big]+A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}
and

H_{1}=-2+4LC_{a}+A\big[1+2C_{a}\big]2C_{0}\theta_{0}>0
.
Then using (
7.48
) and (
7.51
),

\frac{{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{+}}{{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}}\leq L\quad\impliedby\quad\frac{\lambda+H_{0}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}}{\lambda-H_{1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}}\leq L\,.

This later inequality is satisfied as soon as

(7.52)

\lambda\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\frac{H_{0}+LH_{1}}{L-1}\,.

To guarantee that
\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h
satisfies the second cone condition (
7.4
), we need

(7.53)

|W|^{-q}\frac{|\int_{W}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi|}{\fint_{W}\psi}\leq A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,.

for all
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
and
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
.
Using (
7.45
) and (
7.46
), the left hand side is bounded above by

(7.54)

|W|^{1-q}\lambda+|W|^{-q}\frac{|\int_{W}{\mathcal{L}}h\psi|}{\fint_{W}\psi}\,.

Changing variables as in (
7.43
), we estimate the contribution from long pieces
precisely as in (
7.45
). Yet the contribution from short pieces must be done with
care since
W
itself may be short. As in (
7.46
), we estimate,

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}&\int_{W_{i}}h\,(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+B_{i})-B_{i}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\int_{W_{i}}h\\
&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}A\delta^{1-q}|W_{i}|^{q}\fint_{W_{i}}(\psi\circ T\,J_{W_{i}}T+2B_{i})\\
&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big]\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}|W_{i}|^{q}|\psi|_{C^{0}}|J_{W_{i}}T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}\,.\end{split}

Now
|\psi|_{C^{0}}\leq e^{a(2\delta)^{\beta}}\fint_{W}\psi\leq 2\fint_{W}\psi
so that,

(7.55)

\begin{split}\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\!\!\!\!\frac{|\int_{W_{i}}h\psi\circ TJ_{W_{i}}T|}{|W|^{q}\fint_{W}\psi}&\leq 2{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\frac{\big[1+2C_{a}\big]}{\delta^{q-1}}\hskip-6.0pt\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\frac{|W_{i}|^{q}}{|W|^{q}}|J_{W_{i}}T|_{C^{0}(W_{i})}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}4A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big]\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\frac{|TW_{i}|^{q}}{|W|^{q}}\frac{|TW_{i}|^{1-q}}{|W_{i}|^{1-q}}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}4A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big]\left(\sum_{i\in S_{1}(W)}\frac{|TW_{i}|}{|W_{i}|}\right)^{1-q}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}4A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big]\left(C_{0}\theta_{0}\right)^{1-q}\end{split}

where in the third line we have used the Hölder inequality together with the fact that

\sum_{i}\frac{|TW_{i}|}{|W|}\leq 1
, and in the last line we have again used the one-step
expansion from
[
8
, eq. (3.4)]
.

Putting this estimate together with the estimate on long pieces yields an upper bound
for the left side of (
7.53
) (using the fact that
|W|\leq 2\delta
),

(7.56)

\begin{split}(2\delta)^{1-q}\lambda&+{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\Big((2\delta)^{1-q}\big[1+8C_{a}\big]L+4A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big](C_{0}\theta_{0})^{1-q}\Big)\\
&=:2^{1-q}\delta^{1-q}\lambda+\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}H_{2}\,.\end{split}

On the other hand, we obtain a lower bound on the right side of (
7.53
)
using (
7.51
)

A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\geq A\delta^{1-q}(\lambda-H_{1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

This together with (
7.56
) implies that (
7.53
) is satisfied provided

2^{1-q}\delta^{1-q}\lambda+\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}H_{2}\leq A\delta^{1-q}(\lambda-H_{1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

The above holds true provided,

(7.57)

\lambda\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\frac{H_{2}+AH_{1}}{A-2^{1-q}}\,.

Remark 7.18
.

Although
{\mathcal{L}}h
may not be in the cone, the estimates (
7.54
) and (
7.56
)
together prove that for all
W\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
and all
\psi\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(W)
,

\left|\int_{W}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi\right|\leq\fint_{W}\psi|W|^{q}\delta^{1-q}H_{2}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,

which will be useful in what follows.

It remains to choose
\lambda
large enough to satisfy the third cone condition (
7.5
).
For this we need,

(7.58)

\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\,\delta^{1-\gamma}cA{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\,,

for
W^{j}\in{\mathcal{W}}^{s}_{-}(\delta)
and
\psi_{j}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\alpha}(W)
with
d_{*}(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})=0
.
As before, using (
7.51
)
the right hand side of (
7.58
) is bounded below by

(7.59)

d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}cA(\lambda-H_{1}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.

We proceed to obtain an upper bound for the left hand side of (
7.58
).
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}=1
and
|W^{2}|\geq|W^{1}|
.
Also, we assume

(7.60)

|W^{2}|^{q}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}c\delta^{q-\gamma}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\,.

Otherwise, applying (
7.4
) to both terms separately and using (
7.48
), we obtain,

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|&\leq 2|W^{2}|^{q}A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}\\
&\leq\tfrac{1}{2}cAd_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\delta^{1-\gamma}(\lambda+H_{0}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-})\,.\end{split}

This, together with (
7.59
) implies that (
7.58
) holds whenever

\lambda\geq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}(H_{0}+2H_{1})
.

We proceed to prove the upper bound under the assumption (
7.60
). Now,

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq\left||W^{1}|-|W^{2}|\right|\lambda+\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\\
&\quad\leq\left||W^{1}|-|W^{2}|\right|\lambda+\frac{\left|\int_{W^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{2}\right|}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\left|\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}-1\right|+\left|\int_{W^{1}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{2}\right|\,.\end{split}

Using (
7.35
), (
7.36
) and
Remark
7.18
, we can write

(7.61)

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq\left|\int_{W^{1}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{2}\right|\\
&\hskip 24.0pt+\lambda 2C_{s}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})+\left(\frac{\delta}{|W^{2}|}\right)^{1-q}6C_{s}H_{2}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\\
&\hskip 12.0pt\leq\left|\int_{W^{1}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{1}-\int_{W^{2}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{2}\right|\\
&\hskip 24.0pt+\delta^{1-\gamma}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\left(2C_{s}^{q}H_{2}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}+2\lambda C_{s}\right)\,,\end{split}

where in the last line we have used (
7.60
) together with
d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})\leq\delta
,

\gamma\leq q<1/2
and the constraint
c\geq 16C_{s}^{q}
from (
7.6
) (see
[
8
, eq. (5.7)]
for more details).

It remains to estimate the difference in integrals in (
7.61
). For this, we change variables as usual
and integrate on elements of
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\delta}(W^{k})=\{W^{k}_{i}\}_{i}
, the homogeneous connected components
of
T^{-1}W^{k}
, with long pieces subdivided to have length between
\delta
and
2\delta
. As in
Section
7.10
and following
[
8
, Sect. 5.2.3]
, we subdivide elements of

{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(W^{k})
into matched and unmatched pieces by defining a foliation of vertical line segments

\{\ell_{x}\}_{x\in W^{1}_{i}}
centered at
x
of length at most
3C_{1}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})

such that their images under
T
either terminate on a singularity curve in
{\mathcal{S}}_{-1}^{{\mathbb{H}}}
or else
are unstable curves having length at least

d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})
on either side of
T(x)\in W^{1}
. In the latter
case, either
T(\ell_{x})
intersects
W^{2}
, or by the uniform transversality of stable and unstable
curves, lies within distance
C_{2}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})
of an endpoint of
W^{1}
, where

C_{2}
depends only on the minimum angle between stable and unstable curves.
When
T(\ell_{x})
intersects
W^{2}
, then necessarily
\ell_{x}
intersects an element of

{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\delta}(W^{2})
. We call subcurves for which all points are connected by such vertical line segments ‘matched.’ The rest we call ‘unmatched.’

With this identification, we may label elements of
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\delta}(W^{1})
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}^{\delta}(W^{2})

so that each element of
{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\delta}(W^{1})
contains at most one matched subcurve and
at most 2 unmatched subcurves. We will use the decomposition

{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\delta}(W^{k})=(\cup_{j}U^{k}_{j})\cup(\cup_{j}V_{j}^{k})
, so that
U^{1}_{j}
and
U^{2}_{j}
are matched
and so recalling (
7.1
), are defined as the graphs of functions
G_{U^{k}_{j}}
over the same

r
-interval
I_{j}
for each
j
. Using this decomposition, we write

\int_{W^{k}}{\mathcal{L}}h\,\psi_{k}=\sum_{j}\int_{U^{k}_{j}}h\,\psi_{k}\circ T\,J_{U^{k}_{j}}T+\sum_{j}\int_{V^{k}_{j}}h\,\psi_{k}\circ T\,J_{V^{k}_{j}}T\,.

We estimate the contribution to (
7.61
) from unmatched pieces first.

As noted previously, an unmatched curve in
V^{k}_{j}
has image satisfying

|T(V^{k}_{j})|\leq C_{2}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})
.
Choosing
B_{i}
as in (
7.44
), we estimate the sum on
V^{k}_{j}
using (
7.55
) to obtain,

(7.62)

\begin{split}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j}\int_{V^{k}_{j}}h\,\psi_{k}\circ T\,J_{V^{k}_{j}}T&\leq{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}A\delta^{1-q}\big[1+2C_{a}\big]\\
&\hskip 12.0pt\times\sum_{k=1}^{2}\sum_{j}|\psi_{k}|_{C^{0}}|J_{V^{k}_{j}}T|_{C^{0}(V^{k}_{j})}|V^{k}_{j}|^{q}\\
&\hskip-24.0pt\leq 6A\delta^{1-q}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}C_{2}^{q}d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{q}\sum_{j}|J_{V^{2}_{j}}T|^{1-q}_{C^{0}(V^{2}_{j})}\,,\end{split}

where we have used the fact that since

, we have

,
and also
|\psi_{2}|_{C^{0}}\leq 4
as in

.
Since

and there are at most 2 curves

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{\int_{W^{1}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{1}}{\fint_{W^{1}}\psi_{1}}-\frac{\int_{W^{2}}(\lambda+{\mathcal{L}}h)\psi_{2}}{\fint_{W^{2}}\psi_{2}}\right|\leq\frac{2d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\gamma}\left(C_{s}^{q}H_{2}{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}h{|\>\!\!|\>\!\!|}_{-}+\lambda C_{s}\right)}{\delta^{\gamma-1}}\\

We claim that this suffices to complete the proof of Proposition

As in the proof of Corollary

, for

, we have

h+\|h\|,\|h\|\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\cup\{0\}
. Thus with

chosen as above for the element

, and since

, we may write,

where we have used
{\mathcal{L}}1=1
. Then since both terms on the right belong to

,
so does

. Now replacing

, and since

, we have

. We conclude that

,
which completes the claim and the proof of

with

.

Proof of Sublemma
7.19
.

(a) We would like to simply apply the second cone condition (
7.4
) to the integral
in part (a), but
\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})
is not necessarily a valid test function.
Following (
7.44
), it suffices to choose

a^{-1}H^{\beta}(\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2}))+|\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{0}}\leq D_{j}\leq|\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{\beta}(U^{1}_{j})}

in order to guarantee that
\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})+D_{j}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{a,\beta}(U^{1}_{j})
.
We proceed to estimate

.

First, from
[
8
, eq. (5.18)]
it follows that,

(7.66)

|\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{0}(U^{1}_{j})}\leq Cd_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha}|\widehat{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{0}(U^{2}_{j})}\,.

Then by standard estimates (see, for example, the proof of Sublemma
7.17
or

, Lemma 4.3]
), we conclude

|\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{\beta}(U^{1}_{j})}\leq 3d_{{\mathcal{W}}^{s}}(W^{1},W^{2})^{\alpha-\beta}\max\{C|\widehat{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})|_{C^{0}},H^{\beta}(\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})+H^{\beta}(\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2}))\}\,.

However, since

and

and

) yields,

for some uniform

. We will use this as our upper bound on

.

With this bound established, we complete the proof of (a) using the second cone condition

),

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{U^{1}_{j}}h(\widehat{T}_{U^{1}_{j}}(\psi_{1})-\widetilde{T}_{U^{2}_{j}}(\psi_{2})+D_{j})\right|+\left|\int_{U^{1}_{j}}hD_{j}\right|\\

where we have used the previous bound

.

) to bound the difference in curve lengths.

.

Appendix A
Complexification

Proof of Lemma
2.5
.

We follow
[
37
, Section 1 (b)]
. We consider the real vector space
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}^{2}
and, for
(x,y)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{k}^{2}
, define the multiplication by a complex number
a+ib\in{\mathbb{C}}
by

(A.1)

(a+ib)(x,y)=(ax-by,ay+bx).

One can check directly that this defines a complex vector space. Next, we define the norms

\begin{split}&\|(x,y)\|_{r,k}=\sqrt{\|x\|_{k}^{2}+\|y\|_{k}^{2}}\\
&\|(x,y)\|_{c,k}=\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi]}\|e^{i\theta}(x,y)\|_{r,k}.\end{split}

Note that, since
30
30

30

The second inequality follows from

\begin{split}\sqrt{(\|x\|_{k}^{2}+\|y\|_{k}^{2})(\|w\|_{k}^{2}+\|z\|_{k}^{2})}&\geq\sqrt{\|x\|_{k}^{2}\|w\|_{k}^{2}+\|y\|_{k}^{2}\|z\|_{k}^{2}+2\|y\|_{k}\|w\|_{k}\|x\|_{k}\|z\|_{k}}\\
&=\|x\|_{k}\|w\|_{k}+\|y\|_{k}\|z\|_{k}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\|(x,y)+(w,z)\|_{r,k}&\leq\sqrt{\|x\|_{k}^{2}+\|w\|_{k}^{2}+\|z\|_{k}^{2}+\|y\|_{k}^{2}+2\|x\|_{k}\|w\|_{k}+2\|y\|_{k}\|z\|_{k}}\\
&\leq\|(x,y)\|_{r,k}+\|(w,z)\|_{r,k}\end{split}

\|\cdot\|_{r,k}
is a norm for
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}^{2}
and with such a norm
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}^{2}
is obviously a real Banach space. On the other hand,
\|e^{i\theta}(x,y)\|_{r,k}
is a continuous function of
\theta
, hence there exists
\theta_{*}
such that

\begin{split}\|(x,y)+(w,z)\|_{c,k}&=\sup_{\theta\in[0,2\pi]}\|e^{i\theta}[(x,y)+(w,z)]\|_{r,k}=\|e^{i\theta_{*}}[(x,y)+(w,z)]\|_{r,k}\\
&\leq\|e^{i\theta_{*}}(x,y)\|_{r,k}+\|e^{i\theta_{*}}(w,z)]\|_{r,k}\leq\|(x,y)\|_{c,k}+\|(w,z)\|_{c,k}.\end{split}

In addition, for all
\rho\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+},\varphi\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\|\rho e^{i\varphi}(x,y)\|_{c,k}=\sup_{\theta}\|\rho e^{i\theta}(x,y)\|_{r,k}=\rho\|(x,y)\|_{c,k}=|\rho e^{i\varphi}|\|(x,y)\|_{c,k}.

That is
\|\cdot\|_{c,k}
is a norm for the complex vector space
{\mathbb{B}}_{k}
. Note that, by equation (
A.1
), it is natural to write the elements of
{\mathbb{B}}_{k}
as
x+iy
,
x,y\in{\mathcal{B}}_{k}
.

Since the norm
\|\cdot\|_{c,k}
dominates the real norm, we have that
{\mathbb{B}}_{k}
is a complex Banach space. We have then the canonical extension of
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
defined as
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(x+iy)={\mathcal{L}}_{k}x+i{\mathcal{L}}_{k}y
.
In addition,

\|x+iy\|_{c,k}\leq\|x\|_{c,k}+\|y\|_{c,k}=\|x\|_{r,k}+\|y\|_{r,k}\leq\sqrt{2}\|x+iy\|_{r,k}\,,

which implies

(A.2)

\|h\|_{c,k}\leq\sqrt{2}\|h\|_{r,k}.

To conclude the proof note that

\begin{split}\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(x+iy)\|_{c,k+m+1}&=\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}x+i{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}y\|_{c,k+m+1}\\
&\leq\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}x\|_{c,k+m+1}+\|i{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}y\|_{c,k+m+1}\\
&=\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}x\|_{k+m+1}+\|{\mathcal{L}}_{k+m}\cdots{\mathcal{L}}_{k}y\|_{k+m+1}\\
&\leq C_{*}(\|x\|_{k}+\|y\|_{k})\leq C_{*}\sqrt{2}\|(x,y)\|_{r,k}\\
&\leq C_{*}\sqrt{2}\|x+iy\|_{c,k}.\end{split}

∎
